# 263: Chapter 264: Dylan’s Recommendation

In the depths of Viridian Forest, two legendary Pokémon faced off.

However, it was clear that one side's fighting spirit seemed somewhat lacking at the moment—yes, that would be Mewtwo.

"Peppa."

"Mii~!"

'Yveltal' lifted its head and let out a piercing cry, as surging black energy slowly seeped from its body, surrounding it in a dark aura.

This was Yveltal's ability—Dark Aura!

Under the influence of Dark Aura, Yveltal's Dark-type moves became even stronger.

It was an incredibly powerful ability, and not just for itself—Dark Aura provided a group-wide boost, meaning Yveltal could extend its power to other Dark-type Pokémon as well.

Unfortunately, its signature move, Oblivion Wing, was not a Dark-type attack.

As Dark Aura spread, Yveltal's presence grew even more sinister and terrifying—it looked every bit like a final boss.

"Senior Giovanni, aren't you going to attack first?" Dylan asked curiously.

Given that Mewtwo's moves had been sealed and that Peppa had transformed into Yveltal, Dylan figured it was only fair to let Giovanni strike first.

This was his way of being kind, but Giovanni showed no intention of attacking, which puzzled Dylan.

"..."

How exactly do you expect me to attack?

Giovanni felt that Dylan had absolutely no sense of fairness. Mewtwo normally had an extensive move pool with excellent coverage.

But right now? Every single one of Mewtwo's moves had been sealed—except for Psystrike.

Giovanni had heard of Yveltal before—it was said to be a legendary Pokémon from Kalos, known for its Oblivion Wing, a harbinger of death, the bringer of life's end.

And now that he was seeing it in person, Giovanni was 100% sure that Yveltal was a Dark-type legendary Pokémon.

Which meant Mewtwo's only remaining move was completely useless—because Yveltal was immune to Psychic-type attacks!

Giovanni was beginning to think this battle was pointless—Mewtwo had no chance of winning, especially with its moves sealed.

"Mewtwo, do you still want to continue?" Giovanni asked, respecting Mewtwo's choice.

"I want to try." Mewtwo replied, a hint of frustration in its voice.

Logically, Mewtwo knew it had zero chance of victory, but it just couldn't accept it.

It had already lost to Mew once—was it really going to forfeit this time? That wasn't like Mewtwo!

At the very least, it had to try!

"If that's what you want—go, Mewtwo!"

Giovanni let out a small chuckle, regaining his usual composed and steady demeanor as he gave the command.

Mewtwo gripped its massive psychic spoon and charged toward Yveltal with the determination of a warrior marching to their doom.

"Oblivion Wing." Dylan smiled radiantly at the response.

He had actually worried that Giovanni and Mewtwo might surrender outright.

But now? He could finally use Peppa in battle, and as Yveltal no less! He had to make the most of it!

Time to try out Oblivion Wing!

"Mii~!"

'Yveltal' let out another sharp cry as its massive wings folded inward, its entire body pulsing with eerie crimson energy.

The next moment, a massive red beam of energy shot forward—Flying-type Oblivion Wing, Yveltal's signature move.

This was an attack infused with the power to steal life itself!

The beam moved insanely fast—Mewtwo's expression grew tense as it quickly raised its spoon in defense, forming a barrier of blue Psychic energy.

BOOM!

The Oblivion Wing slammed into Mewtwo's barrier, forcing it backward through sheer impact.

With a loud explosion, the crimson energy detonated, sending shockwaves and winds surging through the sky.

The sky turned a deep blood-red, reflecting in Lillie's and Truebird's wide eyes.

From within the crimson storm, Mewtwo burst forth, still encased in its psychic shield.

"Dark Pulse!"

Yveltal once again folded its sinister wings, black energy surging and condensing around it as Dark Aura intensified.

A pulse of black energy shot forward.

"I can't block that!"

Mewtwo's instincts screamed danger—if it tried to block with a psychic barrier, it would be shattered on impact. Dark Pulse was a Dark-type attack, meaning it had a natural advantage against Psychic-types.

Without hesitation, Mewtwo shot upward, narrowly avoiding the attack as it continued closing in on Yveltal.

Yveltal, however, didn't move. It didn't need to.

Because right now, Mewtwo posed absolutely zero threat.

'Yveltal' honestly couldn't understand why this rebellious child was still trying to fight. Was this just a phase?

Mewtwo finally got into melee range, swinging its psychic spoon with full force at Yveltal's side.

BAM!

Yveltal lifted its right wing, effortlessly blocking the attack.

And… that was it.

Since the spoon attack wasn't a move, it was just a regular physical hit—not even enhanced by Mewtwo's psychic power.

Yveltal felt some pain, but… that was about it.

"Dark Pulse!"

The dark energy around Yveltal surged again.

At point-blank range, Mewtwo had no way to dodge.

BOOM!

The Dark Pulse exploded against Mewtwo's body, sending it flying backward with a cry of pain.

The attack had done massive damage—Dark-type moves were already super-effective against Psychic-types, and Dark Aura had made it even stronger.

"That's enough, Dylan. We concede."

Giovanni spoke up firmly.

"There's no point in continuing this fight—Mewtwo cannot defeat Yveltal."

His words weren't just for Dylan—they were also for Mewtwo.

Mewtwo had managed to steady itself, breathing heavily. The last Dark Pulse had done a number on it.

The logical part of its mind understood—it had lost.

Without a word, Mewtwo descended, landing behind Giovanni.

"I acknowledge it as well, Dylan—you are stronger than me."

Giovanni looked at Dylan with admiration. Even if he had gone all-out, he still would have lost.

And Dylan was still so young. The future ahead of him was terrifying.

At this point, Giovanni couldn't even imagine who could challenge him—would he have to fight aliens next?

"Just luck." Dylan responded humbly.

Meanwhile, the white glow of transformation enveloped 'Yveltal,' rapidly shrinking until it reverted to a chubby Clefairy, which promptly snuggled back into Dylan's backpack.

"Heh… no, it's far beyond luck now."

Giovanni chuckled before glancing at the Clefairy poking its head out of the backpack.

"Truly powerful… it seems Mewtwo still has a long way to go before surpassing Mew."

So that was the real challenge.

Mewtwo's name literally meant "Surpassing Mew."

But now, it seemed that dream was still far away.

"Peppa is strong—it's been around long enough."

Dylan shrugged.

"Shall we return and talk?" Giovanni suggested.

"Sounds good." Dylan nodded, curious about what Giovanni wanted to discuss.

"Peppa."

Back at the Viridian Gym

In an instant, Peppa used Teleport once more, bringing everyone back to Viridian Gym.

They returned to the same office, where Truebird resumed making tea.

Mewtwo stood by the floor-to-ceiling window, gazing out at Viridian City. No one knew what it was thinking, but its posture carried a deep, contemplative air.

"Dylan, you should have figured it out by now—my true identity."

Senior Giovanni leaned back on the sofa, his voice as deep and magnetic as ever. His composed and mature demeanor had returned, exuding authority. Though his features still carried a hint of sharpness, they did not evoke any sense of malice.

Lillie pursed her lips and turned to look at Dylan. Her brother had definitely realized it too.

"The boss of Team Rocket, right? Senior Giovanni."

Dylan took a sip of tea, looking at Giovanni with a half-smile.

"That's correct." Giovanni nodded calmly.

"I never would have guessed—the strongest Gym Leader in Kanto, praised by so many, turns out to be the very source of Team Rocket's darkness."

Dylan's eyes gleamed with intrigue as he spoke.

"If this news were to spread, Kanto would be shaken to its core. I bet Senior Lance would storm in and reduce your Viridian Gym to rubble."

Giovanni chuckled at Dylan's words, unfazed.

"And what's your reason for revealing this to me? You must know, I'm currently called the 'Rocket Hunter'—I've personally taken down quite a few of your top executives."

"That's true."

Giovanni nodded with a faint smile. This meeting, and his decision to lay everything bare, was something he had carefully considered.

"But Dylan, do you truly want to destroy Team Rocket?"

Giovanni's gaze sharpened as he locked eyes with Dylan.

"Or rather, what is your real reason for opposing Team Rocket? Is it out of a sense of justice?"

"I'm a League Gym Leader. Do I need a reason to go after Team Rocket?" Dylan countered.

As the Leader of Rota Gym, he was naturally aligned with the Kanto League. He didn't need any extra justification to fight Team Rocket.

"But the League isn't as clean as you think, Dylan. A clever person like you must realize that." Giovanni remarked.

"Perhaps you believe the League stands for righteousness, but the truth is, this world is far darker than it seems. Many people commit crimes under the League's name."

Giovanni accepted a file from Truebird and pushed it toward Dylan and Lillie.

"Take a look at this—see just how 'clean' the people within the League truly are."

"Poaching, illegal dealings, even murder. And do you know how many high-ranking League officials have ties to Team Rocket?"

Giovanni's smile carried a hint of sarcasm.

Dylan flipped through the document briefly before passing it to Lillie, who immediately started reading.

The deeper she read, the more shocked she became. Her worldview was being shaken to its core.

"I already know this." Dylan, however, remained unfazed.

As he had pointed out before, the Pokémon League had dominated the world for over a century. By now, it was the sole superpower.

With an organization that large, corruption was inevitable. There would always be parasites and hidden dangers lurking within.

The Shijiana Incident was a prime example—why had it caused barely a ripple? Because the Johto Pokémon Association was directly under the jurisdiction of the Kanto League, and more importantly, it was a department with real power.

Even someone like Goodshow, the League President, or Lance, the Champion, had no idea about it. And that was just one case.

"But even so, the League must continue to exist. Senior Giovanni, while the League has its flaws, it is still far better than Team Rocket."

Dylan did not mince words, nor did he hide his hostility toward Team Rocket.

The reason Team Rocket was universally despised in Kanto was simple: they stole other people's Pokémon and conducted horrific genetic experiments on innocent Pokémon.

That was a crime that could never be justified.

Dylan enjoyed excitement and interesting situations, but he also loved Pokémon. The idea of using them for genetic experiments was something he could never accept.

"Perhaps you're right." Giovanni was not surprised by Dylan's stance. He had never considered himself a good person. He never bothered dressing up his ambitions with flowery excuses.

"Victory determines everything. In the end, there will be a final outcome. Dylan, the reason I invited you here today is to propose a deal."

Giovanni's sharp gaze locked onto Dylan.

"So, Senior Giovanni, you've decided to settle things with the League once and for all?" Dylan immediately understood.

Over the past six months, under the League's relentless crackdown, Team Rocket in Kanto was on the verge of collapse.

Giovanni must have realized this and was preparing for his final move.

"That's right. But with you in the picture, Team Rocket has no chance of winning." Giovanni admitted.

"Senior Giovanni, you must be joking. Even without me, Team Rocket has no chance of winning." Dylan raised an eyebrow.

"I admit that you are incredibly strong, and with Mewtwo at your side, you have a powerful trump card. But besides you, who else in Team Rocket can fight?"

Dylan pointed out the harsh reality.

Giovanni was undoubtedly a world-class Champion-level Trainer. Combined with Mewtwo, he was a terrifying force.

Aside from Dylan himself and that old man in Johto, no one in the world could match Giovanni one-on-one.

But so what?

This wasn't a one-on-one battle—it was a full-scale war between Team Rocket and the Kanto League.

And what did Team Rocket have beyond Giovanni?

A bunch of nobodies.

Meanwhile, the Kanto League had Lance and the Elite Four, along with two 'wild' Trainers—Red and Blue.

That was six Champion-level Trainers, not to mention that Kanto could request reinforcements from other regions.

Even if Dylan didn't participate, Team Rocket's chance of victory was still zero.

Giovanni was strong, but he was just one man. Even with Mewtwo, he had no chance against six Champions.

A 1 vs 6 battle? That wasn't even a fight—it was a complete beatdown.

"Perhaps… but if you don't interfere, at least there will be a chance."

Giovanni knew the odds, but he refused to give up. Team Rocket was his life's work.

"I won't stay out of it. And to be clear, Senior Giovanni, I won't allow the Kanto League to lose."

Dylan shook his head.

He fully intended to watch this final clash between Team Rocket and the League.

In fact, he had planned to sit back and enjoy the spectacle. Since Team Rocket had no chance of winning anyway, there was no need for him to intervene—it wouldn't be fun.

But he would be there, watching the grand finale.

And if necessary, he would step in to ensure Team Rocket's utter defeat.

The League might have its flaws, but it was still far better than Team Rocket.

"I see." Giovanni sighed internally.

"However, Senior Giovanni, I do have a recommendation for you."

Dylan's eyes gleamed as he suddenly came up with an interesting idea.

"Oh?" Giovanni raised an eyebrow.

"Team Rocket can't win against the League. But as Kanto's most infamous villainous team, shouldn't it at least have a grand exit?"

Dylan's smile was warm and bright.

Giovanni narrowed his eyes.

Had Dylan already seen through his intentions?

He understood that Team Rocket couldn't win. And his own body didn't have much time left.

But if Team Rocket was going to fall, it should be glorious!

To achieve that, he had to ensure that Dylan wouldn't interfere—or at least, wouldn't go all out.

Otherwise, Team Rocket wouldn't even get a dramatic ending.

"Who are you recommending?" Giovanni asked after a pause.

"Aizen. His name is Aizen."

# 264: Chapter 265: This Is Bad!

"Aizen. His name is Aizen."

Dylan smiled as he spoke to Giovanni. That's right—he was recommending his alternate identity to Giovanni.

Come on, why else had he been keeping the "Aizen" persona?

If not for moments like this?

The final showdown between Kanto's Team Rocket and the Pokémon League—this was bound to be entertaining.

And participating in it as Aizen?

Even more fun!

Lillie: (—)

Lillie's delicate face took on a strange expression. She knew full well that "Aizen" was just another identity her brother used.

Ah, this... Was her brother planning to stir up chaos again?

"Aizen… If I remember correctly, he's currently a wanted criminal across all regions of the Pokémon League?"

Giovanni narrowed his eyes slightly, recalling what he knew about Aizen. He had never met Aizen in person, but he had certainly looked into him.

After all, being the first person to ever be officially hunted by every regional Pokémon League was quite the infamous feat.

"Dylan, you know Aizen?"

Giovanni's interest was piqued. According to the League's bounty, Aizen was an extremely dangerous criminal.

And yet, Dylan—a League Gym Leader—claimed to be acquainted with him?

"You could say that. A friend of mine is his partner. Aizen is someone who is always drawn to interesting things and has no taboos."

"More importantly, he is very strong."

Dylan nodded.

Giovanni's gaze flickered. If Dylan was using the words very strong, then Aizen's strength was no small matter.

"A while ago, when Hoenn's Magma and Aqua Teams waged their land-sea war, Aizen and Zinnia were actually the masterminds behind it. Their goal was none other than Rayquaza."

Dylan explained.

"I see." Giovanni mused.

"And in the end, he succeeded. He captured Rayquaza and left. That's why the League has made him their top priority."

Dylan sipped his tea and continued, "Giovanni, I don't mean to underestimate you, but in terms of direct combat strength, Aizen may not yet surpass you. However, if you were to actually fight him, your chances of winning would be very low—even with Mewtwo."

His gaze flickered toward Mewtwo, who turned its head to look at him in return.

"Oh? Because of Rayquaza?" Giovanni's interest deepened.

"Exactly. Rayquaza is incredibly powerful. And Aizen also has a Koraidon—another formidable Legendary Pokémon."

"I see."

Giovanni's eyes glimmered as he looked at Dylan.

"You're willing to help me contact Aizen?"

"Of course. If Aizen joins, then Zinnia will likely come as well. She's a Dragon-type Champion-level Trainer—stronger than Hoenn's previous Champion, Wallace."

Dylan's lips curled into a slow smirk.

"Just to give Team Rocket a grand exit?" Giovanni stared deeply at Dylan.

"Not just that. More importantly…" Dylan tilted his head slightly.

"Isn't this just fun?"

Yes. A massive clash between Champion-level Trainers, all fighting for their own convictions.

That was very interesting, wasn't it?

"Heh…"

Giovanni let out a low chuckle. Compared to that insufferable Lance, Dylan, someone who shared his Viridian Power, was far more agreeable.

"If I survive the battle in Saffron City, Dylan, I'll owe you a favor."

Giovanni took a sip of tea, his voice steady.

He was no saint, but Giovanni never broke his word. Team Rocket had been the center of his life for too long.

If Dylan was willing to give Team Rocket a spectacular finale, then he would remember this debt.

"Here—Articuno and Moltres, captured by my subordinates. They're yours."

Giovanni tossed two Poké Balls to Dylan.

Originally, he had planned to capture all three Legendary Birds for the "Three Birds Fusion" project, using machines to forcibly merge them into one. This fusion would have unleashed incredible power.

But Zapdos had already been caught by Dylan. Keeping the other two was pointless now.

Instead of letting them go to waste, why not gift them to Dylan?

Even though Giovanni knew this gesture hardly repaid the favor Dylan had granted him, it was the only thing he could offer at the moment.

"Giovanni, you're surprisingly generous."

Dylan accepted the Poké Balls without hesitation, rolling them in his palm before glancing back at Giovanni.

"You mentioned Saffron City. Are you setting the battlefield there?"

"That's right. Saffron is Kanto's largest and most developed city. I've chosen it as the stage."

Giovanni nodded.

Saffron City was the economic hub of Kanto, second only to Indigo Plateau in prominence.

"No wonder Archer and the others have been gathering there."

Dylan wore a look of realization.

Giovanni shot him a glance, while Carr, who had been standing silently by his side, twitched slightly.

She subtly glanced at Dylan, already struggling to hold back an internal complaint.

You call Archer 'Mr. Archer'? If you respect him so much, why did you throw him into prison?

And Archer is one of the Boss's most trusted executives! The Boss is right here!

"Since you've chosen Saffron City, what's your plan for Silph Co.?"

"I have no interest in it anymore. Besides, Dylan, aren't you the young master of Silph Co.?"

Giovanni looked at Dylan with slight confusion.

What was he implying?

Was he warning him not to target Silph?

But Giovanni truly had no intention of doing so anymore.

After all, he had discovered that any major fund transfer from Silph Co. required shareholder approval, and the company's largest shareholder, Itou, had veto power.

And Itou just so happened to be Dylan's father.

Upon learning this, Giovanni had abandoned any ambitions toward Silph.

He wasn't about to ruin his relationship with Dylan over money.

"No, no, I think it's worth reconsidering. Giovanni, Silph still has too many scattered shareholders. Team Rocket could 'encourage' them to sell their shares. What do you think?"

Dylan said with a smile.

"Heh… I like the way you think."

Giovanni chuckled softly. He really did find Dylan's company quite enjoyable.

"What about Edward?"

Giovanni took another sip of tea. Edward was the current acting president of Silph Co.

"Edward is a decent person. Let's leave him alone."

Dylan shook his head. Silph Co. still needed a leader, and Edward was a respected elder in his family.

"No problem. Leave it to me."

For Team Rocket, this kind of operation was routine.

For Giovanni, it was just a minor favor.

"Then that's settled. Giovanni, I'll go contact Aizen now."

Dylan stood up, ready to leave.

"Take care."

Giovanni personally escorted Dylan and Lillie to the door, watching as their figures disappeared from view.

Then, he lowered his gaze to the two gleaming stones in his palm—

The Mega Stone and Key Stone for Beedrill, a gift from Dylan before he left.

"Boss, can Dylan really be trusted?"

Carr stood behind Giovanni, adjusting her glasses as she asked.

"There's no reason for him to deceive me."

Giovanni replied calmly.

Dylan's strength was far greater than his own. If he had truly been loyal to the League, he could have subdued him back when Beedrill appeared.

"Carr, start making preparations. Your identity has always remained clean. Once this is all over, you can begin a new life."

Giovanni's voice was steady.

Carr was not a Trainer; she had always been responsible for Team Rocket's management.

"The time is drawing near."

Giovanni gazed at the distant horizon, where the setting sun cast long shadows.

The sun is setting…

"...Understood." Carr was silent for a moment before bowing her head in response.

"Do we need to inform Lady Sabrina?"

"Sabrina, huh… Let her continue being a Gym Leader."

Giovanni shook his head. Sabrina was a powerful Elite Four-level Trainer and a formidable Psychic-type specialist.

But in the battle ahead, even her strength wouldn't be enough.

He had no intention of letting her go down with Team Rocket. She still had a future beyond this.

"Carr."

"Yes, Boss."

"After the battle in Saffron City, visit Archer and the others for me."

"Understood."

Carr's eyes reddened slightly, but she lowered her head and responded firmly.

"Tell them… to cooperate with the League and secure an early release. They still have a future."

"…Yes."

—

Leaving the Viridian Gym, Dylan and Lillie exited Viridian City and took off on Reshiram, heading toward Rota Street.

"Brother, is it really okay to help Team Rocket? What if the League finds out?"

Lillie sat in Dylan's arms, resting her head against his chest, her voice tinged with worry.

She wasn't afraid for his safety, but if the League discovered that Aizen was actually Dylan, it would undoubtedly cause major trouble.

"Don't worry. Leaf's imitation skills are top-notch. Just stick by her side and make sure she doesn't stir up too much trouble."

Dylan replied casually. After his time in Hoenn, he had finally realized the best way to use Leaf.

That woman was the perfect decoy.

When the time came, he would hand Reshiram, his little turtle, and Kyurem over to her. She could parade around with them, attracting attention, while he—disguised as Aizen—could have fun playing with the League's top trainers.

"Alright…" Lillie thought about it and realized he had a point. Back in Hoenn, even Steven and Cynthia hadn't noticed anything.

"But with Giovanni and Aizen both on Team Rocket's side, won't they be too strong?"

Lillie tilted her head.

Giovanni was already as strong as Lance, a true Champion-level Trainer. Now, with Beedrill's Mega Evolution Stone, his already terrifying Beedrill would become even stronger. And then there was Mewtwo, his trump card.

Meanwhile, Aizen had Koraidon and Rayquaza, making him an even greater threat than Steven and Cynthia.

"No, it's still not enough." Dylan mused.

"Huh?" Lillie blinked.

"Zinnia has to join in. And so does Karen. If I offer her a Rainbow Wing, she'll take this battle very seriously."

Dylan's eyes gleamed. He wanted to orchestrate a large-scale, thrilling war.

Normally, Karen would just slack off—or even flee from the battlefield altogether.

But with a Rainbow Wing as an incentive?

She'd definitely fight seriously.

Luckily, he had plenty of those. That little cutie Mew had plucked a whole bunch from Ho-Oh's feathers.

Of course, getting Karen to fight to the death would be tough. She was too clever for that.

"That makes four Champion-level Trainers on Team Rocket's side. Isn't that way too strong?"

Lillie was stunned. That meant Team Rocket had four Champions!

"If you don't even go all out, Champion Lance and the others won't stand much of a chance…"

"Which is why we need more players in the game."

Dylan thought for a moment, then pulled out his phone and dialed a number.

"Hello? Dylan? What's the matter?"

A warm, gentle voice—the voice of President Goodshow—came through the receiver.

Dylan's tone suddenly became grave.

"President Goodshow, this is bad! I just got word—"

"Aizen has joined Team Rocket! They're planning a coup!"

# 265: Chapter 266: Calls for Reinforcements!

"What did you just say?"

President Goodshow had been smiling moments ago, but now his expression froze in shock.

Aizen—wasn't he that criminal from Hoenn who tamed Rayquaza?

Now he was in Kanto? And he had teamed up with Team Rocket?!

President Goodshow shot up from his seat. This was terrible news.

"Dylan, are you sure about this?" he asked urgently.

"Absolutely. This intel is solid—I wouldn't be mistaken."

Dylan's voice was firm, as if he were swearing on his life.

"Dylan, I need you to come to Indigo Plateau immediately."

President Goodshow furrowed his graying brows.

Team Rocket was already difficult to handle.

Their leader was an exceptionally strong trainer—even Lance had fought him twice without securing a victory. The two were evenly matched.

And now Aizen was joining them? That was real trouble.

"Right now?"

"Yes, this is too important. I know it's a hassle, but please."

"Of course, Mr. President. You know me—I have zero tolerance for criminal organizations." Dylan's voice was warm and sincere.

"We're lucky to have you, Dylan."

President Goodshow was genuinely moved.

With Dylan on their side, what did Kanto have to fear?

Yes, he had overreacted. Rayquaza and Aizen? So what?

I have General Dylan! What is there to be afraid of?

"Come straight up to my office when you arrive. I'll contact Lance and the others so we can discuss our strategy."

With that, President Goodshow ended the call and immediately began summoning the Kanto Elite Four.

—

"Looks like we're not going back anytime soon, Lillie."

Dylan chuckled. He hadn't expected President Goodshow to take this so seriously.

"Brother, you're so good at this."

Lillie rolled her eyes playfully, covering her mouth as she giggled.

"Alright, Reshiram—let's go."

With a graceful turn, the majestic white dragon changed course, soaring toward Indigo Plateau.

—

The League's headquarters wasn't far from Viridian City.

After about half an hour, Reshiram descended outside the towering Pokémon League building.

"Gym Leader Dylan, the President asked me to wait for you."

A secretary greeted Dylan respectfully at the entrance.

"Please, lead the way."

"Right this way, both of you."

The secretary bowed slightly and led them upstairs to the President's office.

After knocking, she gestured for Dylan to enter.

Pushing open the door, Dylan found that not only was President Goodshow inside, but the entire Kanto Elite Four was present.

"Mr. President, Lance, Lorelei."

Dylan smiled as he greeted them.

Lance gave him a brief nod, arms crossed, eyes closed, as if deep in thought.

Lorelei motioned for Dylan and Lillie to take a seat on the couch across from them.

"Agatha, Bruno—it's an honor to finally meet you."

Dylan turned to the two remaining members of the Elite Four.

Bruno sat shirtless, his muscular arms folded, his gaze sharp and focused as he studied Dylan.

Meanwhile, Agatha leaned on her cane, her wrinkled face breaking into a cryptic smile.

"Hehehe… Dylan, I've heard so much about you. You're quite an impressive young man. Tell me, how's that Gastly of yours?"

Despite her friendly tone, Agatha's aura was unsettling—years of working with Poison and Ghost-types had given her a rather eerie presence.

"So that shiny Gastly really was yours, Agatha? Its talent is outstanding—thank you for the gift."

Dylan raised an eyebrow. He had suspected as much.

"Hehehe… No need to thank me. I knew it would be in good hands."

Agatha's smile remained… unnervingly kind.

—

"Alright, Dylan—tell us everything you know about Aizen."

President Goodshow sat down beside him as the secretary served tea to everyone.

"Aizen is allegedly from Unova. He's Champion-level—strong enough that even when Steven and Cynthia teamed up against him, he only barely lost."

"He then seized an opportunity to capture Rayquaza and escaped. I wasn't able to stop him at the time."

Dylan's expression grew serious.

Lance opened his eyes, finally paying attention.

"How strong are Steven and Cynthia?" he asked.

Since Wallace had retired, Steven was the current Champion of Hoenn, while Cynthia held the Champion title in Sinnoh.

Lance had never fought them himself, so he wasn't sure how they compared.

"They're both extremely powerful Champions."

Dylan's answer was clear.

Lance's sharp brows furrowed slightly.

If Dylan said they were strong, then there was no doubt about it.

It seemed Champions from other regions weren't just weaklings.

"And how do you know Aizen joined Team Rocket?" Lorelei asked skeptically.

Dylan sighed before replying, "Zinnia told me."

"Zinnia?" President Goodshow rubbed his temples. He had heard of her before.

"She was my childhood friend," Dylan admitted. "But our ideals no longer align. She and Aizen are now working together."

"So she's with Team Rocket, too?"

President Goodshow's headache worsened.

Agatha, however, looked puzzled.

"Who is this Zinnia?" she asked.

"She's a descendant of the Draconid people and a Champion-level Dragon-type specialist. Due to certain circumstances, she no longer trusts the League."

Dylan's voice was calm.

Agatha's brows furrowed.

"So now there are two Champion-level trainers in Team Rocket… and Aizen has Rayquaza."

Even she had to admit—it was a problem.

"Three Champions," Lorelei corrected, arms crossed.

Team Rocket's leader was already as strong as Lance. And now they had two more?

"Actually, it's four." Dylan spoke up again.

"The new Rocket executive, Karen—she's a Dark-type Champion. I've fought her twice, and I can confirm it."

"She's a high-level Champion, too. She's probably on par with Lorelei or Bruno."

Dylan glanced at Bruno.

Yep. According to the original storyline, Karen had definitely surpassed Bruno.

Now there were four Champion-level trainers in Team Rocket.

Even Lance was frowning now.

How did they lose so many high-ranking officers… and still keep getting stronger?

Over the past six months, the League had been crushing Team Rocket, taking out their low-level members by the thousands.

But those grunts didn't matter. The real issue was the high-level leadership—the true power of Team Rocket.

"And don't forget—Team Rocket also has Mewtwo."

Dylan's tone was heavy.

"A Mewtwo?"

President Goodshow's expression darkened.

"Yes. A Psychic-type Legendary Pokémon, at least as strong as Reshiram. Its origins are unknown, but it appeared during the last battle in Saffron City to rescue Karen and Will."

The room fell silent.

"We need reinforcements," Dylan stated.

"Let's wipe Team Rocket out for good."

Dylan turned to Lorelei and said, "Capturing Champion-level trainers isn't easy. The only way to do it is through careful preparation or sheer numbers."

Lorelei was momentarily stunned by his words, but then she silently nodded—Dylan was right.

"He's absolutely right," President Goodshow agreed, making the final call. "This time, we will completely eradicate Team Rocket in Kanto."

His voice was firm.

"That tumor has been growing in Kanto for over ten years. It's time to cut it out for good."

Although Team Rocket had a presence in Johto as well, their operations there were nowhere near as strong. In Johto, the organization was mostly led by the three Beasts.

Once they eliminated Team Rocket in Kanto, the Johto branch would be easy to handle.

Lance and the other three Elite Four members had no objections. As long as Team Rocket was destroyed, that was all that mattered.

—

With the discussion over, the group prepared to leave.

"Agatha, I heard that your Arbok can enhance its abilities by changing the patterns on its belly. Would you mind teaching me a thing or two?"

As Agatha stood up to leave, Dylan approached her with his question.

Agatha's Secret Art—Death Makeup intrigued him. Though it couldn't be used to alter patterns mid-battle, Dylan wasn't looking to copy it entirely.

Agatha paused, then chuckled. "A curious youngster, aren't you? Very well, follow me."

It was the first time anyone had asked her about Death Makeup.

Arbok wasn't a particularly popular Pokémon—aside from criminals, few trainers used it as a main battler.

After all, its appearance—deep purple, large, and cobra-like—was unsettling to many. On top of that, it wasn't the strongest Pokémon out there.

"Thank you, Agatha."

Dylan was respectful.

If he could adapt her Death Makeup technique and train his Arbok to modify the patterns on its own chest…

Wouldn't that be like precise pressure point targeting? That could be incredibly useful.

He valued Arbok highly—it was one of the veteran Pokémon he had personally caught.

Spending a good amount of time with Agatha, Dylan carefully listened as she explained the principles of Death Makeup and the different enhancements tied to each pattern.

Agatha was impressively generous—she didn't hold back a single detail while teaching him.

She was truly an outstanding and reliable senior.

After bidding her farewell, Dylan couldn't help but think—it was a shame that Agatha and Professor Oak never got together back in the day.

If they had, Blue's grandmother would have been Agatha.

—

By the time Dylan and Lillie returned to the Rota Gym, night had already fallen.

Dylan headed straight to the teleportation room—Old Den had sent over some Pokémon again.

There was a note attached:

"One of these took quite a bit of effort to get."

Dylan raised an eyebrow. None of the Pokémon he had requested were that rare, were they?

Taking the three Poké Balls in hand, he walked to the pond in the backyard.

"Come on out."

With three flashes of white light, three Pokémon appeared before him.

Dylan smiled gently. "Hello there. I'm Dylan—I'll be your trainer from now on."

The first Pokémon was a small, water-blue creature with a slightly oversized head, orange gill-like markings on its cheeks, and a tall fin on its head.

—

'Beep—Mudkip. Water-type. The Mud Fish Pokémon.

Ability: Torrent.

Moves: Water Gun, Growl, Tackle, Mirror Coat (Egg Move).

Pokédex Entry: The fin on its head acts as a highly sensitive radar, allowing it to sense its surroundings through water or air currents—even without using its eyes.'

—

That's right—it was Mudkip!

Its final evolution was Swampert, a Pokémon that Wallace had used before.

Dylan was confident that his future Swampert would one day surpass Wallace's.

Besides, Swampert could Mega Evolve. That was a huge advantage.

—

Next to Mudkip was a small, white seabird with elongated wings and a long, slender beak—Wingull.

—

'Beep—Wingull. Water/Flying-type. The Seagull Pokémon.

Ability: Hydration.

Moves: Wing Attack, Supersonic, Quick Attack, Water Gun, Soak (Egg Move).

Pokédex Entry: It has a habit of carrying food and important items in its beak, storing them in various locations. It glides effortlessly on the wind.'

—

Dylan nodded in approval.

A Hydration Wingull would evolve into a Drizzle Pelipper—perfect for setting up rain.

Very nice.

Then, he turned his gaze to the last Pokémon, his eyes gleaming with excitement.

—

The third Pokémon was a light blue, frog-like creature with large, yellow eyes. White, foamy bubbles covered its mouth, back, and neck.

—

'Beep—Froakie. Water-type. The Bubble Frog Pokémon.

Ability: Torrent.

Moves: Quick Attack, Water Gun, Pound, Growl, Counter (Egg Move).

Pokédex Entry: It produces bubbles from its chest and back. These bubbles are elastic and help reduce damage from attacks.'

—

That's right—this Pokémon was Froakie!

Dylan was taken aback.

Not only was it a Froakie—but it wasn't a normal one.

Typical Froakie were a deeper shade of blue.

But this one was a lighter shade…

An alternate-colored Froakie!

No wonder it said it took effort—this was a shiny Pokémon.

And if Dylan remembered correctly, Shiny Greninja was an incredibly cool-looking black!

# 266: Chapter 267: Rota’s New Member!

Froakie—its final evolution was Greninja, a Water/Dark-type Pokémon.

It was the Water starter from the Kalos region and one of the most popular Pokémon ever. Why?

Because Greninja was Ash's ace Pokémon during his journey in Kalos.

Their deep bond allowed Greninja to undergo a special transformation—the Ash-Greninja form.

Of course, in the Lumiose Conference finals, Greninja had an "unfortunate" loss against Alain's Charizard X, costing Ash the championship.

Greninja had been incredibly strong throughout the tournament, but this particular battle left fans… well, frustrated.

Thus, the infamous meme "Water Storage Charizard" was born.

Still, Dylan personally believed that Pikachu was the true MVP of that fight.

Pikachu had defeated Alain's Tyranitar and Metagross—both pseudo-Legendaries—before finally losing to Charizard.

"You three need to grow strong, okay?"

Dylan crouched down, smiling at the three young Pokémon—Froakie, Mudkip, and Wingull—who were either looking at him curiously or shyly.

Under the influence of his Viridian Power, their affection toward him skyrocketed.

"I wonder if my Greninja will be able to transform into Ash-Greninja?"

Dylan glanced at his shiny Froakie, deep in thought.

In order for Greninja to Bond Transform, it needed the Battle Bond ability.

In the games, Battle Bond Greninja couldn't be obtained through normal means—only through special events.

But reality was different.

Would this shiny Froakie naturally develop Battle Bond upon evolving?

—

It was already late, so Dylan washed up and went to bed.

—

Growth Simulation—Start!

Sinnoh Region, Lily of the Valley Island.

The headquarters of the Sinnoh Pokémon League.

This was the destination for all trainers who had endured countless trials and tribulations, seeking to stand at the pinnacle of battle.

Dylan had already made it here.

He had even defeated Sinnoh's Elite Four.

Now, he stood before the final door—the Champion's Chamber.

The room was cast in dark gray hues.

This was Cynthia's domain.

"So, you've arrived, Dylan."

As Dylan stepped into the room, a tall woman dressed in a black, form-fitting coat turned to face him.

Cynthia's golden eyes gleamed with warmth as she gave him a poised smile.

"How have you been? Oh, and I should thank you for resolving the crisis at Spear Pillar."

Cynthia spoke gracefully.

"No matter the challenge, you and your Pokémon can overcome it. That unwavering spirit is what allows you to surpass your limits."

Dylan listened silently, not interrupting.

After all, this wasn't the real Cynthia—just an AI-controlled Cynthia, following pre-set dialogue.

"Thanks to you, I've learned something important."

Cynthia's smile deepened as she took her position at the battlefield.

"Now then! I know why you're here. As the Pokémon League Champion, I will face you in battle!"

Her presence shifted.

Despite being a woman, she exuded an aura of absolute authority as she raised her hand.

Cynthia, the Sinnoh Champion, has challenged you to battle!

—

And then…

"You were the strongest challenger I've ever faced."

Cynthia recalled her last Pokémon—her fallen Garchomp.

She looked at Dylan with admiration.

"That was a beautiful, truly spectacular battle!"

"You commanded your Pokémon to their fullest potential, made clear decisions, and fought with composure—securing a flawless victory."

"Passion and strategy, combined into one. No matter where you go or what obstacles you face, you will always find a way to overcome them. Battling you has shown me that truth."

"Sinnoh has a new Champion!"

Cynthia's praise was overflowing, but Dylan simply found it… boring.

This wasn't the real Cynthia.

Just a scripted AI spouting lines.

Finally, after what felt like an eternity of dialogue, Dylan entered the Hall of Fame.

—

'Champion Team Recorded: Mewtwo, Metagross, Ludicolo, Gengar, Greninja, Swampert.'

—

Dylan sighed.

Welp.

On his way here, Greninja and Swampert had managed to evolve by leeching experience.

That wasn't the problem.

The problem was that, since Legendaries weren't normally available in the first run, he had no choice but to use Mewtwo.

But in reality, he was confident that he could get the real Mewtwo.

After all, Giovanni hadn't actually captured Mewtwo yet.

Still, getting Mewtwo through the Growth Simulation was fine.

He just wondered—when he suddenly had a "twin," how would Rocket's Mewtwo react?

—

'Ding—Congratulations! You have become the Sinnoh Champion and entered the Hall of Fame for the first time.'

'You may select one Pokémon from your Champion Team to materialize in the real world.'

A translucent screen appeared before Dylan.

"I choose Mewtwo."

There was no hesitation.

After all, Mewtwo was his only option this time.

"Confirm selection?"

"Confirm."

'Selection successful. Mewtwo will now be transferred to reality.'

Perfect.

Now he had his own Mewtwo.

Dylan waited, expecting another notification… but nothing came.

"So, clearing Sinnoh alone doesn't unlock the Hisui region?"

Dylan frowned slightly.

He had assumed that unlocking Hisui—the ancient Sinnoh—would be tied to completing the modern Sinnoh region.

But that wasn't the case.

Had he missed some kind of condition?

He had to reach Hisui eventually.

In modern Sinnoh, there was no way to obtain the Azure Flute—the key to summoning Arceus.

The only way to get it was in Hisui.

"I'll focus on capturing the Sinnoh trio first."

Dylan put the thought aside.

If the system refused to tell him how to unlock Hisui, he'd have to figure it out himself.

Did he need to clear every region first?

Either way, he'd move on to Johto next.

After clearing that, he'd bring back one of Sinnoh's Legendaries.

—

The Next Morning

Dylan woke up, washed up, and stepped into the backyard.

"Come out."

He tossed a Luxury Ball.

A flash of white light, and—

A humanoid Pokémon with a sleek white body, a long green tail, and piercing eyes appeared before him.

Mewtwo.

"It's a Shiny one."

Dylan examined his newly acquired Mewtwo.

Its body was nearly identical to the one Team Rocket had.

But while Rocket's Mewtwo was purple, his Mewtwo's tail and belly were green.

A Shiny Mewtwo.

"How do you feel?" Dylan asked.

"I feel… excellent."

Mewtwo's deep, resonant telepathic voice echoed in his mind.

Dylan blinked.

The voice…

It sounded slightly feminine.

Just like the Mewtwo from Genesect and the Legend Awakened.

"Try this out, Mewtwo."

Dylan took out a crystal-clear stone—the Mega Evolution Stone for Mewtwo.

This was the same stone given to him by Dr. Fuji.

"Do you need me to activate it with my Key Stone?"

"No need. The energy inside… is the same as mine."

Mewtwo's deep blue eyes pulsed with Psychic energy.

It could feel the stone's resonance.

Mewtwo clenched its fist around the Mega Stone—

A dazzling evolutionary light burst forth.

Mega Evolution!

The light faded, revealing a new form—

Its body was smaller but more streamlined.

Large, ear-like extensions curled from its head, and a long tail-like appendage stretched from its skull.

Mega Mewtwo Y.

"How does it feel now?" Dylan asked again.

This time, the meaning was different.

Mega Mewtwo Y didn't need a trainer to trigger its transformation.

Just like in the movie.

"Amazing! Dylan, I can feel… an endless surge of power!"

Shiny Mewtwo Y's calm telepathic voice carried a hint of amazement as it raised both arms, gazing at its newly evolved form.

Though its body had become smaller, Shiny Mewtwo Y could feel an overwhelming surge of power coursing through it!

Beep~ Shiny Mewtwo Y, Psychic-type, Genetic Pokémon.

Ability: Insomnia

Moves: Psystrike, Recover, Aura Sphere, Thunder, Blizzard, Fire Blast, Nasty Plot...

Description: Though its body has shrunk, its power has grown immeasurable. With just a slight use of its psychic abilities, even skyscrapers could be reduced to ashes. (Shiny Mega Mewtwo Y)

The Rotom Pokédex displayed Shiny Mewtwo Y's information, and Dylan nodded in satisfaction. Not bad at all!

"This Mega Stone is yours now, Mewtwo," Dylan said.

A flash of light enveloped Shiny Mewtwo Y as it reverted to its normal form. Without hesitation, it absorbed the Mega Stone into its body, ensuring that it could Mega Evolve whenever it wished.

Since today was Sunday, the Rota Gym was closed, meaning Dylan didn't have to open for business.

Initially, Dylan planned to take Lillie out for breakfast, but as soon as they stepped out of the gym's entrance—

"Huh? Brother, look over there," Lillie pointed toward the corner near the gym, her voice filled with curiosity.

Dylan followed her gaze and raised an eyebrow.

"What are you doing here?"

He and Lillie walked over, looking curiously at the red-haired boy standing in the corner, lost in thought.

"It's none of your business," the boy replied coldly.

He was a sharp-featured youth with pale skin and messy red hair. His aura was overwhelmingly cold and distant, making it clear he wasn't the type to welcome company.

This guy seems really unfriendly…

Lillie frowned slightly. He was standing right outside their home—how could it have nothing to do with them?

At this point, Lillie had already subconsciously started seeing the Rota Gym as her home.

"Oh? You're not here to see me? Silver?" Dylan asked, intrigued as he examined the red-haired boy—Silver.

Silver's expression changed instantly. He instinctively stepped back, his body tensing like a wary beast sensing danger.

But he quickly regained his composure, his gaze turning doubtful as he looked at Dylan.

"You are… Dylan?"

Silver wasn't foolish—he was actually quite sharp.

"Leaf sent you to find me, and yet you don't even know what I look like?" Dylan sighed.

"And why are you so late? Leaf told me you should've arrived within two or three days."

"…"

Silver pressed his lips together and remained silent.

Grrr~

Suddenly, an awkward grumble echoed from Silver's stomach. His head dropped lower, his ears turning red as he felt Dylan and Lillie's amused gazes on him.

"I… lost my phone and wallet on the way here…" he muttered.

"Seriously?"

Dylan sighed and rubbed his forehead. Oh well.

"Come with me. Since you're Leaf's little brother, that makes you my little brother too. Let's grab some food first, then you can get some rest—you probably haven't had a proper sleep in a while, right?"

He patted Silver's frost-covered clothes, realizing the boy must have been standing outside for quite some time.

Silver instinctively flinched at the touch, reluctant to let others get too close—likely a habit from his rough upbringing. But he remembered what his sister, Leaf, had said: Dylan is someone you can trust completely.

And besides, he could sense genuine kindness from Dylan.

"I woke up early this morning," Silver muttered stubbornly.

"If you got here so early, why didn't you knock on the door?"

"…" Silver remained silent. He wasn't good at this kind of thing.

Dylan took Silver and Lillie to a nearby breakfast shop and ordered Silver a large bowl of noodles.

"Eat slowly. If you're still hungry, I'll get you another bowl. Afterward, we'll go buy you some new clothes."

Dylan smiled warmly at Silver, while Lillie curiously observed her new acquaintance—Leaf's younger brother.

"…Thanks," Silver said hesitantly. He stopped eating for a moment, seemingly struggling to get the words out, before finally murmuring his gratitude.

"No need to be polite. From now on, you'll be my apprentice—Leaf told you about that, right?"

"Yeah."

Silver nodded. He was still skeptical about whether Dylan could truly stand against the Masked Man, but he trusted Leaf. And so far, he was starting to trust Dylan as well.

After breakfast, Dylan took Silver shopping for clothes before returning to the Rota Gym.

When Silver stepped out after changing into his new outfit and tidying up his hair, he looked like a completely different person.

Dressed in a fitted deep-purple high-necked long-sleeve and matching purple pants, his now neatly combed red hair accentuated his sharp, defiant demeanor. The transformation made his cold and rebellious aura even more pronounced.

"Not bad, looking sharp," Dylan said, eyeing Silver with approval.

"…" Silver looked away, rubbing his nose awkwardly. He wasn't used to this kind of praise.

But for the first time—aside from Leaf—he felt a warmth from someone else.

"From today onward, you're part of the Rota Gym, Silver. Welcome aboard."

Dylan grinned and patted Silver's shoulder.

He planned to train Silver well.

And when Silver was ready, Dylan would send him to challenge Giovanni.

Oh, I can't wait to see Giovanni's face when that happens.

It was going to be interesting—after all, Silver was Giovanni's ultimate counter.

(Note: To have get access for 20 chapters ahead go to my patreon and be a member ✨🌟)

Patreon: patreon.com/RockyRoad891

# 267: Chapter 268: Invincible Ahead!

A strange ceiling.

When Silver woke up again and saw the unfamiliar yet lavishly decorated ceiling, he froze for a moment. His eyes quickly sharpened with caution, but he soon remembered.

Ah, right—he had come to the Rota Gym. His sister Leaf had introduced him, and he had officially become Dylan's apprentice.

Still not used to the environment, Silver got up, changed clothes, quickly washed up, and stepped out of the room.

"You're up? Big Brother Dylan said to head to the backyard and find him once you were awake."

Hearing the movement, the little girl—currently binge-watching dramas with Morgrem—called out to Silver.

"Okay," Silver responded flatly and turned to head toward the backyard.

"He doesn't seem very easy to get along with…"

Seeing how cold Silver's attitude was, the little girl tilted her head, a bit troubled. After all, he was Dylan's new apprentice and Leaf's little brother.

Oh well, they'd warm up to each other in time.

When Silver stepped into the backyard, he was greeted by an expansive, field-like courtyard.

Many Pokémon were frolicking across the grass. Some were sparring. Some he recognized, some he didn't.

"Teacher."

Silver scanned the yard and spotted Dylan sitting beneath a tree. He immediately walked over.

"Hey Silver, all rested up? Looks like you're a lot more energetic now."

Dylan was gently stroking the Eevee napping in his arms, deep in thought. Upon hearing Silver's voice, he looked up and smiled.

"Thank you for your concern," Silver replied politely, though his tone remained chilly.

"I told you, no need to be so formal," Dylan said, a little helplessly.

"Leaf told me you already have your own Pokémon, right?"

"Yes."

Silver nodded and released a Pokéball. A dark-colored Pokémon appeared beside him.

Its body was pitch black, with a red tail and a sinister expression. It had two sharp claws.

It was a Sneasel.

"This is my first Pokémon. It's been with me since I was a child," Silver said.

Sneasel had always been his most trusted partner—his first and closest Pokémon.

"Leaf mentioned you two were taken by the Masked Man at a young age. Have you ever thought about looking for your parents?" Dylan asked curiously.

In the Special manga, Silver was only two when he was kidnapped, and Sneasel was taken with him.

But this world was slightly different. Silver was now fifteen, and the kidnapping happened nine years ago—when he was already six. So unlike the manga, his memories shouldn't be entirely gone.

"My memories are blurry. I only vaguely recall that my home was in Kanto, and that I had a father… but I can't remember much more than that." Silver's lips tightened as he shook his head.

It had been nearly ten years. His memories had faded too much. He only remembered rarely being allowed outside as a kid—so much so that he couldn't even recall what city his home was in.

"I'm sorry."

"It's fine. Maybe my family has already forgotten about me. Things are good as they are now," Silver said calmly.

He wasn't like Leaf. He did miss his family, but his indifferent personality kept him from dwelling on it.

After all, it had been so long. He didn't even know where they were anymore. Silver had already stopped hoping.

"Blood ties are something distance and time can't erase. I believe you'll find them," Dylan offered gently.

Silver stayed silent.

Dylan didn't press further. Like his manga counterpart, Silver in this world was cold and aloof on the outside—but deep down, he leaned toward kindness.

In the games, Silver traveled to Johto after arguing with Giovanni, who had been defeated by Red and went into seclusion to train. Silver vowed to grow strong on his own.

In the Special manga, Silver refused to acknowledge Giovanni as his father because of his hatred for Team Rocket. He only accepted Giovanni after the man nearly died saving him. Even then, Silver never forgave Team Rocket.

"You're my third apprentice, Silver. Your senior is Selene, and your second senior is Yellow."

Dylan stood and smiled at Silver.

Silver said nothing. He didn't know Selene, but he had met Yellow that morning—a girl who seemed innocent and gentle. Frankly, Silver wasn't impressed.

To him, Yellow had none of the aura of a strong trainer. And Silver was a proud person. Having someone like her as his senior didn't sit well with him.

Just because someone joined first didn't mean they deserved to outrank him. One day, he'd show Yellow who the real number two was.

No—number one! He'd surpass Selene too!

"Selene left on her journey a while ago. You'll meet her when she returns to the gym."

"Her personality's a bit… unusual, but she's very strong. You should try learning a thing or two from her."

Dylan seemed to read Silver's mind and smiled knowingly.

He understood. Young people had pride and ambition—and Silver was clearly a proud one. Being the third apprentice didn't sit well with him, and that was perfectly natural.

"I've also prepared a little welcome gift for you, since it's your first day as my student."

Dylan took out a Pokéball and released a Pokémon that looked strikingly similar to Delibird.

Its entire body gave off a metallic, mechanical vibe, and beneath its right wing was an orb made of icy crystals.

Silver blinked in surprise. He'd never heard of a regional form of Delibird before.

"This is Iron Bundle, a Water/Ice-type Paradox Pokémon from the future. Think of it as a little welcome gift from me, your teacher."

Dylan smiled.

"Thank you, teacher." Silver accepted without hesitation—after all, this was a Pokémon Dylan had entrusted to him.

"Iron Bundle is pretty sturdy. You'll need to train it well. Its personality is a little quirky, so spend time with it and build your bond. It's not too strong yet—just right for you."

Dylan wouldn't give him an overpowered Iron Bundle right away. Silver had great potential, but unlike Leaf and others like Green, he hadn't been trained by the Masked Man—so his current strength wasn't especially high.

Why give him Iron Bundle, then? First, it was genuinely strong. Second, this one's level was low enough for Silver to handle.

And third—Water and Ice types are perfect counters for Ground types. Yep. Perfectly suited.

"I understand."

Silver took the Pokéball and considered how he should introduce himself to the Iron Bundle, who hadn't moved a muscle since appearing.

"What a beautiful day~"

Dylan stretched lazily—when suddenly, his phone buzzed.

He pulled it out and discovered that somehow, he'd been added to a new group chat.

The one who made the group? Wallace.

Group name: Wallace and His Friends

Water-Loving Artist: Steven said he got an invitation from Chairman Goodshow to help deal with Team Rocket. Did you get one too, Cynthia? (confused.jpg)

Ice Cream Lover: I did. The Sinnoh League mentioned it to me—I already agreed.

Water-Loving Artist: Then why didn't I get one? Did Chairman Goodshow forget about me? (hurt.jpg)

Rock-Loving Excavator: Do you really not know? You're the only champion who got retired after a loss. You're also the first regional champion to become world-famous because of that. You embarrassed all of Hoenn. (smile.jpg)

Water-Loving Artist: Oh~ my god! Steven, are you even my friend? How could you bring up my trauma like that? You've gone too far! I'm furious!

Ice Cream Lover: (eating popcorn.jpg)

Sunniest Guy: (eating popcorn.jpg)

Rock-Loving Excavator: Heh~ If you're really my friend, come take my place at work for a few days! I need a break—I'm drowning in stress!

Water-Loving Artist: I have a performance soon. Going dark.

Dylan couldn't help but chuckle. Goodshow was certainly acting fast—he'd already gotten in touch with Steven and Cynthia. Who knew which other regions he'd reached out to?

Just like that, three more days passed.

Dylan wasn't exactly free during those days—there were plenty of challengers.

For the weaker ones, Dylan let Yellow and Silver handle them. Their strength was just right—not too strong, not too weak.

Under the pressure of constant battles, Yellow and Silver's exceptional potential as trainers began to shine. Their growth was astonishing.

Rivals who had been evenly matched just three days ago were now being completely overwhelmed. That's the kind of monsters they were.

And on this particular night, in the Silph Co. headquarters building in Saffron City...

President Silph was still working late in his office. Lately, their company had been focused on producing Master Balls. Aside from the very first one gifted to Dylan, they had now produced three more.

With the Master Ball's debut, Silph Co.'s stock was once again on the rise, and their reputation was soaring.

Although Poké Ball technology was no longer exclusive to Silph Co., they remained the industry titan.

President Silph was deep in thought about something very important. If this plan worked, Silph Co. would truly take off.

But just then, there was a knock at the office door.

"Come in." President Silph sounded annoyed at being interrupted.

The door opened, and his female secretary nervously stepped in. Silph's brows furrowed.

"Who are you?" he asked, his eyes narrowing at the four figures behind her.

"President Silph, it's an honor."

The leader, a slightly chubby middle-aged man, politely removed his gentleman's hat and gave a courteous bow.

"I realize this is terribly sudden, but I'd like to borrow your company's building for a few days. Would you be so kind?"

The disguised Giovanni smiled faintly as he addressed President Silph.

"There's no need to reach for the alarm, President Silph. The entire building is already under Team Rocket's control."

"You…!"

President Silph's eyes widened in shock. Team Rocket?!

"No need to worry. As long as you cooperate, you won't be harmed. That, I promise."

Giovanni stepped forward with a relaxed stride, adjusting his hat, his voice smooth and magnetic.

Despair flooded President Silph's eyes.

At 9 p.m. that night, Dylan—now in his "Aizen" persona—stood before the floor-to-ceiling window in the Silph Co. president's office, silently watching the most dazzling city in Kanto.

Silph Tower was now entirely under their control.

"How's the view of Saffron at night, Mr. Aizen?"

Giovanni approached with a wine glass in hand, offering another to Dylan.

"It's beautiful," Dylan said, gently swirling the crimson liquid in his glass, "but it's still missing something."

The blood-red reflection flickered in his eyes.

Zinnia lounged in the president's chair, rocking it idly while occasionally glancing at Giovanni and Dylan. Karen sat with her legs crossed, flipping through the latest fashion magazine.

"Are you going to add it yourself?" Giovanni glanced sidelong at Dylan, his gaze deep.

A dangerous smile played across Dylan's handsome face as he stared out at the glittering city.

"Of course. Let's set this city ablaze."

"Let it burn!"

They would use Saffron City as the stage for the final battle between Team Rocket and the Kanto League.

"Before us... there are no enemies!"

(End of Chapter)

# 268: Chapter 269: Help Me!

"I want to report a crime!"

A disheveled female secretary burst into the Saffron City Police Station, so frantic she didn't even know where her shoes had gone.

"And you are…?"

The officer looked confused. She looked familiar—wait, wasn't she the secretary of President Silph from Silph Co.?

"Team Rocket! Team Rocket has invaded Silph Co.! They've taken President Silph hostage!" the secretary cried out.

"What?!"

At the Indigo Plateau, Goodshow received a call from the Saffron City police chief. His gray brows immediately knitted together, his expression darkening.

Team Rocket… Had they really grown this lawless? Taking over the entire Silph Co. building?

They clearly had no regard for the Kanto League anymore!

Goodshow immediately contacted Lance and the others to begin planning a counterattack against Team Rocket.

"Saffron City's evacuation has begun. The Kanto League is moving fast."

At the top floor of Silph Co., President Silph sat bound in a corner, sighing miserably.

Karen stepped into the room and spoke to Giovanni, who sat casually in the president's chair. She'd tried to disguise herself and sneak out for a little shopping trip…

But the streets were nearly empty. Clearly, the League had begun mobilizing—the first step in any operation like this was to evacuate civilians.

"Isn't that exactly what we wanted?" Giovanni replied flatly. That secretary? They'd let her go on purpose.

"Fine, Boss. I'll fight at full strength, but let me be clear—I'll retreat if things get desperate."

Karen shrugged, speaking frankly.

Dylan had personally approached her, offering a Rainbow Wing in exchange for her full cooperation in this battle.

She'd hesitated but eventually agreed. Originally, she'd planned to bail—Team Rocket Kanto Division was clearly going down.

But she'd found no clues on this trip, and Will had ended up in jail…

She couldn't return to the organization empty-handed. The Rainbow Wing would at least make up for her failure.

"Of course. As agreed, your own safety comes first," Giovanni said calmly, his voice as smooth as ever.

"I don't get why you're so dead-set on going head-to-head with the League," Karen said, glancing at him. "But I have to admit—it's kind of fun."

She had to give it to him. She respected Giovanni's strength and leadership.

"Where's that Aizen guy?"

"Out for a walk," Zinnia replied lazily, scrolling through her phone.

Karen glanced at her again, curiosity flashing in her eyes. She was very intrigued by the two "reinforcements"—Aizen and Zinnia.

And where was Dylan?

He was, of course, at the Rota Gym. He still had work to do. Thankfully, with Yellow and Silver around, most challengers were easy to handle.

So he had more time on his hands lately.

"Tch~ Silver's improving fast, huh."

Watching Silver's Golbat defeat a challenger, Leaf rested her chin on her hand, visibly impressed. As expected of her little brother.

"He's got talent. And a lot of battle experience," Dylan nodded. Silver really was Giovanni's son—his progress had been rapid.

He was easily on par with Yellow. In fact, with pure battle experience factored in, Silver was probably stronger—at least when Yellow wasn't using her "cheats."

"Mmhm~ So, when's the League making their move?"

Leaf blinked her big, bright eyes at Dylan.

He had specifically called her back. She'd already been undercover in Silph Tower, tracking Team Rocket's movements.

But she hadn't expected Dylan to pull her into something this big.

"Tomorrow. So I'll have to trouble you a bit, Leaf."

Dylan smiled. Goodshow had already informed him that all outside reinforcements had arrived at Indigo Plateau.

"Tch~ Let me just say, if I get exposed, don't blame me. This stuff is so annoying."

Leaf pouted. Why did she, a cute girl, always have to play guys? And sometimes even do identity swaps?

"You won't. Plus, I'll leave Ice Dragon and the others with you—you'll be fine."

Dylan trusted her completely.

"Heh heh heh~ So, is there a chance I'll get to fight you this time?"

Leaf giggled, but her face quickly lit up with excitement.

Last time in Hoenn, she only got to watch Aizen show off. This time, things would be different, right?

"We'll see what happens. No script this time."

Dylan chuckled.

"After all this is over in Saffron, I've got a reward for you."

"Eh? Really?"

"Of course. Would I lie to you?"

"Hehehe~ Sweet!"

Leaf beamed, excited and curious about what the reward might be.

Her joy was obvious, and Dylan couldn't help but smile as he took out his phone. Someone in the Gym Leader chat was tagging him.

Officer Jenny: Attention all members. The League will launch a large-scale operation against Team Rocket in Saffron City tomorrow. Who wants to join?

Everything was ready. The city was completely surrounded.

Jenny didn't know if there were any moles in the chat, but it didn't matter. Saffron was fully locked down now.

Civilians had been evacuated, especially near Silph Tower. Not even a Pidgey could fly out.

So Jenny sent the message to the group.

Officer Jenny: This is a great opportunity for all of you to show your dedication. Time to wipe out Team Rocket once and for all.

Celadon Gym – Erika: I will not stand idly by in the face of evil! Count me in! (Charge.jpg)

Pewter Gym – Flint: My strength's kinda… never mind, I'll just stay out of the way. (Facepalm.jpg)

Officer Jenny: Don't sell yourself short, Gym Leader Flint. You're more than capable of taking down grunt-level Rocket members. Just follow along. (YouCanDoIt.jpg)

Cerulean Gym – Violet: Is it true, Jenny-sis? Can we go too? We can't fight, but we'll totally cheer and spam 666! (Excited.jpg)

Officer Jenny: Violet… maybe just stay home and wait for good news. (Smile.jpg)

Cerulean Gym – Lily: (Crying.jpg)

Cinnabar Gym – Blaine: Ahaha~ This is a big one, huh. Us old folks will sit this one out.

Viridian Gym – Giovanni: Officer Jenny, when do we begin? As a proud Gym Leader of the League, I, Giovanni, vow to fight evil with all my might! Team Rocket? I'll crush them with my own hands! (ThunderousJustice.jpg)

Rota Gym – Dylan: +1

Saffron Gym – Sabrina: (TiltedHead.jpg)

Officer Jenny: As expected from Gym Leaders Giovanni and Dylan! You two really are Kanto's finest! (Gratitude.jpg)

Dylan couldn't help but laugh. Classic Giovanni—still putting on a show, even now.

But it had been Dylan's idea. Giovanni originally planned to face the League under his real identity.

Dylan had persuaded him to wear a disguise—if he didn't die in Saffron, there might still be hope down the line.

Giovanni had seriously considered it, probably for Silver's sake.

As for his stand-in? Look no further than the master of disguise himself—Petrel.

Leaf still needed Ditto to assist her transformations, but Lambda? He could mimic someone better than the original—on his own.

With Lambda as his double, Giovanni could say, without exaggeration, that he had given his all in the "fight" against Team Rocket.

A storm was coming.

The next morning, Saffron City felt like a ghost town—eerily silent.

In the distance from Silph Co., League personnel were gathered.

The Kanto Elite Four, several Gym Leaders including Dylan, and the outside reinforcements Goodshow had brought in.

Steven, Wallace, and Cynthia—old acquaintances. But Dylan's gaze landed on the one furthest to the side.

She wore a sleek black tailored suit, matching black leather shoes, and had tanned skin. Her long black hair was voluminous, and she wore blue gloves on both hands—elegant and sharp.

She was Geeta, the top Champion of Paldea and also the Chairwoman of the Paldea League.

"Didn't think President Goodshow would bring her in."

Dylan was a little surprised to see Geeta. Goodshow had mentioned he'd invited three powerful reinforcements.

Dylan had assumed Alder from Unova, but Geeta? That was unexpected.

As for why there were four people when only three were invited—well, Wallace had tagged along with Steven.

According to Wallace, how could such a thrilling and dramatic battle be complete without him, the graceful former Hoenn Champion?

Justice-driven Wallace had vowed to bury this evil organization with his own hands!

Ever since the incidents with Team Magma and Team Aqua, Wallace had zero tolerance for criminal organizations—especially since Aizen was here too.

"Hehehe~ The time's about right. Let's begin the assault."

Agatha, acting as the overall commander from the rear base, chuckled in her raspy voice.

Tactics? Who needed tactics? Did they realize how many Champions were here?

A total of nine Champion-level trainers! Even excluding herself, who was staying behind to command, that was still eight Champions!

In a situation like this, why bother with strategy? Just storm the place!

With Agatha's order, the League trainers began moving toward the Silph Co. building.

"The Rocket boss and their top execs are confirmed to be inside. We're going straight in."

Lorelei adjusted her glasses, a cold gleam in her eyes, and took the lead toward the building.

"Dylan, what's the plan?" Steven turned to ask.

"Split up. Silph Co. is huge. If you run into Aizen—uh, the other one—be careful," Dylan replied after a moment's thought.

"Got it."

"When this is over, come hang out in Rota. My treat."

Dylan smiled at them.

"And Champion Geeta—thanks for what you did last time."

Dylan looked at Geeta. Her presence truly exuded grace—no wonder she was Paldea's League Chair.

"You're too kind, Gym Leader Dylan." Geeta offered a faint smile, her voice magnetic and composed.

"Let's talk later. First, let's wipe out this evil known as Team Rocket."

Dylan nodded to them and then led Lillie toward the building from a different direction.

"Let's split up too. Wallace, stay alert."

The three foreign Champions—Steven, Cynthia, and Geeta—exchanged looks and agreed to separate. With their level of power, there was no need to stay together. Each of them could turn the tide alone.

"? Steven, what's that supposed to mean?" Wallace gave his close friend a baffled look. Why single him out for a warning?

What danger could he be in? He was still a Water-type Champion, okay?

Other than Aizen, was there anyone in Team Rocket who could take him down? Don't make him laugh.

Come on, with this many Champions raiding a single organization—what could possibly go wrong?

As long as Dylan held off the other Dylan, Team Rocket was toast. Wallace would crush them with ease.

They were going to win!

Steven and the others split up as well. Lance soared on Dragonite straight toward the top floor of Silph Co.

Meanwhile…

"Well, well~ Wallace, long time no see."

Seventeenth floor of Silph Co. Wallace's face turned pale as he stared at the elegant figure draped in a white coat before him—Aizen.

No way. Why was his luck this terrible?!

"Ever since we parted in Hoenn, I've missed you dearly, Wallace. I never expected fate would bring us together again in Kanto."

Dylan lowered his head with a faint smile, a Poké Ball enlarging in his hand.

"Don't come any closer!"

Wallace took two steps back.

Cynthia, save me! Steven, save me! Dylan, save me!!!

Wallace's hand beneath his white cape furiously tapped at his communicator.

Damn it, this was all Steven's fault with his jinx of a mouth—how did he end up running into the boss right away?!
